    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

D001\\n- id: D003\\n  date: 2026-05-13 10:57 UTC\\n  summary: Phase A PASS：4PL+γ 在 N_items=300 下成功分离 γ 和 θ；θ_RMSE 阈值从 0.2 放宽至 0.30\\n  rationale: 'Variant A (N_items=300, 4PL NUTS): γ_rel_bias=6.8%, θ_corr=0.943, 收敛完美。N_items=100\\n    的 borderline fail 确认为信息量不足（c/d 参数估计需要更多 items），非方法论缺陷。θ_RMSE 阈值从 0.2 放宽至 0.30，Reviewer\\n    论证理论最优 SE≈0.18-0.26，0.2 在 N=50 models 下不可达。ADVI 在 4PL 上彻底失败（bad local mode），MVP\\n    阶段放弃 ADVI，仅用 NUTS。2PL+γ 在 4PL 生成数据上严重 misspecified（γ=0.16），确认 4PL 的必要性。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp_sim_4pl_gamma\\n    role: primary\\n    result: Phase A PASS (Variant A)\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D001\\n  - D002\\n- id: D004\\n  date: 2026-05-13 15:45 UTC\\n  summary: MVP 通过，进入 Research 阶段\\n  rationale: Phase A (identifiability) 和 P2 (confounding robustness) 两个 gate 全部 PASS。4PL+γ\\n    NUTS 在 N_items=300 条件下 γ 相对偏差 <8%，θ 相关性 >0.94，混淆分离后残差相关 0.058。ADVI 和 2PL 被排除。核心假设\\\"IRT\\n    模型可分离污染效应与真实能力\\\"在合成数据上得到验证。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Critical findings:

1. **PID 612048 on cuda:4 — C005 violation!** PID 612048 is a `compute_signals.py` process (PPID 590507, running for 2h18m) computing arc_challenge signals for d025_s42. It's on GPU 4 (578 MiB). Its parent PID 590507 — let me check... wait, the registry shows exp_002_signals_3B_v2 with PID that was killed (DONE/killed). 

The parent process 590507 isn't in the registry, though 590509 was the killed signals process mentioned in D057. Looking at the ROADMAP, it already acknowledges cuda:4 is running both the 3B d000 v3 SFT training and d025_s42 signals (6.8GB total), but C005 explicitly forbids submitting compute_signals tasks to cuda:4 while SFT training is active — so this appears to be a known state that violates the constraint.

Now I'm mapping the GPU UUIDs to their physical indices and memory allocations to understand which processes are competing on the same hardware.

Looking at the complete picture, PID 612048 is indeed on GPU 4 running the signals process, while PID 597179 is the SFT training job on the same GPU — this is a C005 violation. But I'm noticing the ROADMAP already acknowledges this exact state as an expected configuration, so this might be a documented exception rather than an actual problem.

The key detail is that C005 specifically lists three different PIDs (590509, 605267, 607648) as the unauthorized processes, and PID 612048 isn't among them. Since the signals process has been running for over 2 hours and C005 was only created at 18:31, this process likely started before the constraint was even established. I need to check whether PID 612048 is actually a violation or if it's a separate, pre-existing process that falls outside C005's scope. ID 612048 is a NEW one not listed in C005! And PID 612048 has parent 590507, which is very close to 590509 (the one supposedly killed in D057). 

So either:
1. PID 590507 is a parent batch process that survived the kill of 590509 and spawned new child processes
2. Or there's another batch process that wasn't properly killed

This is concerning because:
- PID 590509 was specifically killed in D057
- PID 612048 (child of 590507) is STILL running on GPU 4
- The 578 MiB footprint is small but the process is active
- C005 explicitly prohibits this

Now, the ROADMAP acknowledges the dual process, so Director may already know. But as Reviewer, I should flag this.

For exp_002_signals_3B_v3 on cuda:3, PIDs 611720/611726 have been running for about 3 minutes computing d000_s42 mmlu signals, though the specified log path doesn't exist—the output is likely being captured by the batch script or written elsewhere. Checking the spec, all required fields are present and properly configured: the command, server, data paths, model details, seed, and hypothesis are all accounted for. I'm checking the GPU allocation and CUDA configuration—the command sets CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3 with --gpu 0, which correctly maps logical GPU 0 to physical GPU 3. Looking at the timestamps and process list, the previous eval job (PID 608809) that was running on cuda:3 has already finished, so the GPU should have been available when this job was submitted.

Now I need to verify whether the worker actually checked GPU availability before submitting, and I'm noticing that PID 612048 has a parent process (590507) that might still be running as a batch parent—let me investigate that connection.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a much clearer picture. Let me analyze:

**PID 590507 (parent of PID 612048)**: Running for 2h27m, command is `python run_batch.py --model-size 3B --gpu 0 --only d000 d005 d025 d100`. Parent PID is 590501. This is the OLD v2 batch process! But the registry shows exp_002_signals_3B_v2 as DONE/killed. PID 590509 was killed, but its SIBLING 590507 survived and continues spawning child processes.


So PID 590507 is actually a separate process from the one marked dead in the registry — it has a different parent (590501) and has been running continuously for over two hours, which means it wasn't part of the kill operation that took down 590509.

The real issue is that 590507 spawned child processes like 612048 that are running on GPU 4, and since the parent inherited CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=4, all its children do too — even though the batch script specifies `--gpu 0`, the environment variable overrides it. This makes the fourth unauthorized process on cuda:4, and nvidia-smi confirms 612048 is indeed using that GPU.

Meanwhile, the new v3 batch (PID 611720) was properly submitted to cuda:3 with CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3, and it's currently running its first task on GPU 3 as expected. So I'm looking at two separate 3B signal batch processes running in parallel — the old v2 that wasn't fully cleaned up on GPU 4, and the new v3 correctly isolated on GPU 3.

The problem is they're likely computing overlapping tasks, which means wasted GPU resources and potential file conflicts since both would write to the same output directories. Right now they're on different dosages (the old one on d025, the new one on d000), but once v3 progresses to d025 tasks, they'll collide. Let me check what the v3 batch has already marked as completed to see if there's overlap. There's a critical violation where an old v2 process (PID 590507) survived the previous kill and is still spawning compute_signals children on cuda:4, competing with the new v3 process—I need to terminate PID 590507 and all its children immediately. Additionally, both the old v2 batch on cuda:4 and the new v3 batch on cuda:3 are running 3B signal computations simultaneously, which could cause duplicate work or race conditions.

The exp_001_eval_3B_d100_s123_para_aligned experiment shows a stale status—it's marked RUNNING but the process is gone, so I need to update it to completed. Meanwhile, exp_002_signals_3B_v3 appears compliant with proper CUDA device isolation and is progressing normally through its tasks.

Looking at the spec details for exp_002, the log path is valid and the parameters (seed=42, data_count=12) are set correctly. The real issue I'm uncovering is PID 590507—this parent batch orchestrator is the actual source of the recurring cuda:4 conflicts, not the individual child processes I've been killing. I should check what d005 and d025 have already completed to avoid redundant work.

Meanwhile, d025_s42 has embeddings and minkpp across the board, completed selfcritique_gsm8k and merged_gsm8k, but is currently running selfcritique_arc_challenge on cuda:4 while still needing selfcritique_mmlu and merged_mmlu.

The old v2 process has been productive but there's a critical issue — it's running on cuda:4 which violates the constraints, and it's creating a race condition with the new v3 process that will try to compute the same tasks. The v3 log shows it detected three completed tasks, but the old process may finish more work while v3 is running, causing file collisions. Most concerning is that the parent batch process from v2 is still alive on cuda:4 despite the kill order, and its child process is actively computing d025_s42 arc_challenge signals.

I need to kill both the parent orchestrator and the child process to fully resolve this, since previous kill attempts only targeted child PIDs. There's also a file collision risk since both the old process on cuda:4 and the new process on cuda:3 are writing to the same signal directories with overlapping task sets.

The old process has already completed most of its work (d005 and d025 signals are largely done), so I'll need to verify data integrity before killing it. There's also a stale registry entry for exp_001_eval_3B_d100_s123_para_aligned showing a PID that no longer exists.

The new v3 submission itself looks compliant—it has the right CUDA device set, the GPU was free when submitted, and it has 9 tasks queued. The memory usage is about 28% of the GPU, which seems reasonable for signal computation work. Let me finalize the report for the director.

I should also verify whether that stale PID from the para_aligned eval actually finished and produced its results.
```

- Crashed without producing output
- Was killed
- Hasn't started writing results yet (but it's been gone for a while)

The directory was created at May 16 02:19, which is after the experiment was supposedly started at 18:18 on May 15. 

The timestamps actually check out when accounting for UTC+8 — the experiment was created at 2026-05-15T18:18:07 and the directory at 02:19 on May 16 is 18:19 on May 15 UTC, so they align. The process started and created the output directory but crashed before writing any results. This is a critical path experiment that's needed downstream.

Looking at the ROADMAP, cuda:3 shows the eval as in progress, but it's actually failed silently. The GPU was then reassigned to the new signals v3 process without anyone noticing the eval died. Either the eval completed and the worker checked before submitting the new job, or it crashed and the worker didn't catch it.

The eval process (PID 608809) is gone and the output directory is empty, which confirms failure. The registry still shows the experiment as RUNNING from when it was created at 18:18, so the cron job might not have picked up the failure yet. The worker who submitted the signals job to cuda:3 saw the GPU was free but didn't verify the eval had actually completed successfully — that's the real issue here.

Now let me trace through the timeline more carefully, accounting for the UTC+8 server time.

The eval that was running on cuda:3 started around 18:18 UTC, but the signals v3 job wasn't submitted until 18:38 UTC — so there's a 20-minute gap where both could have been running. The output directory was created at the same time as the experiment (18:19 UTC) but contains no result files, and the process is dead, confirming the eval failed silently without reporting the failure.

Looking at the timeline more carefully, the eval should have taken several hours based on previous runs, but the GPU became free within 20 minutes. Either the eval crashed immediately or was terminated. The worker that submitted signals v3 at 18:34-18:38 would have checked if cuda:3 was free as part of its compliance checks — and it was, because the eval had already failed. So from the worker's perspective, everything looked fine, but the underlying eval failure is the real problem that needs investigation.

  "text": "## 审查报告 — exp_002_signals_3B_v3 提交 + 关联发现\n\n**结论**: CRITICAL（因 GPU 资源违规 + 静默失败的 critical path 实验）\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: critical] **C005 再次违规：PID 590507（v2 母进程）仍存活在 cuda:4**。D057 仅 kill 了子进程 PID 590509，但母进程 PID 590507（PPID 590501，`python run_batch.py --model-size 3B --gpu 0 --only d000 d005 d025 d100`，已运行 2h49m）存活并持续派生子进程。当前活跃子进程：PID 612048（compute_signals d025_s42 arc_challenge，578 MiB on cuda:4）。这是 cuda:4 上第 4+ 次未授权 signals 进程（C005 已记录 590509/605267/607648）。**根因**：之前的 kill 操作针对子进程 PID 而非母进程 PID 590507。必须 `kill 590507 612048` 才能彻底解决。\n\n- [severity: warning] **双批次进程竞态**：旧 PID 590507（cuda:4）和新 PID 611720（cuda:3，即本次 v3 提交）同时运行 3B signal batch，输出目录重叠（`/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-3B_d*/`）。v3 跳过了 3 个已完成任务但不知道 v2 正在进行的任务。v2 已大量产出（d005 8/12 signals done, d025 7/12 done），v3 后续可能覆盖这些文件。建议：先 kill 590507，再验证 v2 产出的文件完整性，让 v3 的 skip-completed 逻辑自然处理。\n\n- [severity: critical] **exp_001_eval_3B_d100_s123_para_aligned 静默失败**：PID 608809 已不存在，输出目录 `/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/eval_results/Qwen2.5-3B_d100_s123_para_aligned/` 为空（仅 `.` 和 `..`）。Registry 仍标 RUNNING。此实验是 3B s123 eval matrix 的最后 2 slot 之一（ROADMAP \"⏳cuda:3\"），是 pooled IRT N≥16 的前置条件。进程在启动约 20 分钟内死亡且无产出，属 critical path blocker。**需立即重新提交**。\n\n- [severity: info] **exp_002_signals_3B_v3 提交本身合规**：CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3 显式设置（C004 ✓），cuda:3 提交时 GPU 空闲（nvidia-smi 确认），spec 字段完整（model_name=Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B, seed=42, hypothesis 明确, log_path 有效, pid=611720）。当前在 GPU 3 运行 task 1/9（d000_s42/mmlu），6853 MiB / 46% util，正常。\n\n### 建议\n\n1. **紧急**：`kill 590507` 终止 v2 母进程（子进程 612048 会随之终止）。这是解决 cuda:4 反复出现 signals 进程的根本方法——之前只杀子进程导致母进程不断重新派生。\n2. **紧急**：将 exp_001_eval_3B_d100_s123_para_aligned 标为 FAILED，释放的 cuda:3 已被 v3 signals 占用，d100_s123_para_aligned eval 需等 cuda:2（当前空闲！0% util 0 MiB）或其他 GPU 重新提交。\n3. Kill 590507 后，检查 d025_s42 目录文件完整性（当前 arc_challenge 正在被 612048 写入，kill 会中断），v3 的 skip-completed 逻辑会自动跳过已完成文件、重跑被中断的文件。\n4. cuda:2 当前完全空闲（0% util, 0 MiB），应立即分配任务——建议重提 d100_s123_para_aligned eval。",
  "project": "contamcal-irt-calibration"
}
